\mathrm{TD}\left(\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{O}\leftarrow\mathsf{HP}_{n,2T+1}\end{subarray}}\!\left[\ket{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}}_{t}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}}_{t}}_{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{AB}}}}\right],\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{O}\leftarrow\mu\end{subarray}}\!\left[\ket{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}}_{t}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}}_{t}}_{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{AB}}}}\right]\right)\leq\frac{2t(11t+20)}{N^{1/8}}\kern 5.0pt.

This theorem establishes the statistical strong security.
Assuming post-quantum secure
OWF
s,
it then implies the existence of computationally
strong
PRU
s.
The proof of this theorem follows a similar routine to
Theorem
4.2
:

•

We first use the purification to establish that
making queries to
H_{f}\cdot P_{\sigma}
is equivalent to
querying
\mathsf{HPO}
.

•

We then show that
the adversary cannot distinguish between
making queries to
D\cdot\mathsf{HPO}\cdot C

and querying a path-recording oracle
V
introduced in
Section
5.3
.
Here
C
and
D
are sampled from either
\mathsf{HP}_{n,T}

or Haar distribution
\mu

to ensure that
the environment state is mostly within the distinct block subspace.
We require two unitary operators to achieve this purpose,
as the adversary can make both forward and inverse queries.

•

Finally, we apply the above indistinguishability result to two cases.
If
C,D\leftarrow\mathsf{HP}_{n,T}
, then querying
D\cdot\mathsf{HPO}\cdot C
is equivalent to querying
\mathcal{O}\leftarrow\mathsf{HP}_{n,2T+1}
. On the other hand, if
C,D\leftarrow\mu
, then querying
D\cdot\mathsf{HPO}\cdot C
is equivalent to querying
\mathcal{O}\leftarrow\mu
.
Hence, both query cases can be reduced to the
same path-recording oracle
V
, and
Theorem

5.1
follows from the
triangle inequality.

Similarly to
Theorem
4.6
, we can distill from our proof a stronger security condition for a distribution of unitaries, which approximates Haar random unitary under a larger family of twirling operations. Composing such a distribution of unitaries with an additional round of parallel Kac’s walk yields a strong PRU.

Theorem 5.2

Let
V
be an
n
-qubit unitary sampled from the distribution
\mathsf{HP}_{n,1}
. Define

\Pi^{\mathsf{eq}}:=\sum_{x\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{n}}\ket{x,x}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{x,x}
and

\Pi^{\mathsf{EPR}}:=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{x,y\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{n}}\ket{x,x}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{y,y}
.
Suppose
K
is sampled from a distribution
\mathcal{R}
over
\mathrm{U}\!\left(N\right)
such that

\left\lVert\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}K\end{subarray}}\!\left[A\cdot\Pi^{\mathsf{eq}}\cdot A^{\dagger}\right]\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\delta\quad\text{for all }A\in\{K\otimes K,(K\otimes K)^{\dagger}\}\kern 5.0pt,

and

\left\lVert\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}K\end{subarray}}\!\left[B\cdot(\Pi^{\mathsf{eq}}-\Pi^{\mathsf{EPR}})\cdot B^{\dagger}\right]\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\delta\quad\text{for all }B\in\{K\otimes\overline{K},(K\otimes\overline{K})^{\dagger}\}\kern 5.0pt.

Then, for any adversary making at most
t
forward and inverse queries,

K_{2}VK_{1}
, with
K_{1},K_{2}\leftarrow\mathcal{R}
independently, is indistinguishable from a Haar random unitary except with advantage at most
O(t^{2}\cdot(\delta+1/\sqrt{N})^{1/4})
. Concretely, the following are examples of instantiations of
K
.

•

K
is a
\delta
-approximate unitary
2
-design, or

•

K\leftarrow\mathsf{HP}_{n,T=30n}
is an
O(n)
-round parallel Kac’s walk.

Below, we first extend the
\mathsf{HPO}
oracle and the
\mathsf{HP}
-relation states to handle inverse queries and describe the action of
\mathsf{HPO}
using the
\mathsf{HP}
-relation states in
Section
5.1
.
Then, we introduce a partial path-recording oracle
W
in
Section
5.2

as an intermediate operator to connect the action of

\mathsf{HPO}
and the path-recording oracle
V
defined in
Section
5.3
.
Finally, we prove
Theorem
5.1
in
Section
5.4
.

5.1
Action of
\mathsf{HPO}
Oracle and Its Inverse

We start by adding inverse query to the
\mathsf{HPO}
oracle.

Definition 13

(Purified Function-Permutation Oracle)
.

The
purified function permutation oracle

\mathsf{HPO}
is a unitary
on registers
\mathsf{A}
,
\mathsf{H}
and
\mathsf{P}
, where

•

\mathsf{H}
is a register with Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{H}}}
spanned by the orthogonal states
\ket{f}
for all
f\in\{0,1\}^{n-1}\to\{0,1\}^{3d}
,

•

\mathsf{P}
is a register with Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{P}}}
spanned by the orthogonal states
\ket{\sigma}
for all
\sigma\in\mathcal{S}_{N}
.

The unitary operator
\mathsf{HPO}
acts as

\mathsf{HPO}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{AHP}}}\ket{x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\ket{f}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{H}}}\ket{\sigma}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{P}}}\coloneq\left(H_{f}P_{\sigma}\right)_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\ket{x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\ket{f}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{H}}}\ket{\sigma}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{P}}}\kern 5.0pt,

and
\mathsf{HPO}^{\dagger}
acts as

\mathsf{HPO}^{\dagger}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{AHP}}}\ket{y}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\ket{f}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{H}}}\ket{\sigma}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{P}}}=\left(H_{f}P_{\sigma}\right)^{\dagger}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\ket{y}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\ket{f}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{H}}}\ket{\sigma}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{P}}}\kern 5.0pt.

As in
Fact
4.3
, querying
H_{f}\cdot P_{\sigma}
and querying
\mathsf{HPO}
are identical in the adversary’s view:

Fact 5.3
.

For any adversary
\mathcal{A}
holding the register
\mathsf{A}
, the following two oracles are perfectly indistinguishable:

•

Sample uniformly random
\sigma\in S_{N}
and
f:\{0,1\}^{n-1}\to\{0,1\}^{3d}
.
On each forward query, apply
H_{f}P_{\sigma}

(
(H_{f}P_{\sigma})^{\dagger}
, if it is an inverse query
)
on register
\mathsf{A}
.

•

Initialize registers
\mathsf{H}
and
\mathsf{P}
in the state

\ket{\phi_{\{\}}}\coloneq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{3d(n-1)}}}\sum_{f:\{0,1\}^{n-1}\to\{0,1\}^{3d}}\ket{f}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{H}}}\otimes\frac{1}{\sqrt{N!}}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{N}}\ket{\sigma}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{P}}}\kern 5.0pt.

On each forward query, apply
\mathsf{HPO}

(
\mathsf{HPO}^{\dagger}
, if it is an inverse query
)
on registers
\mathsf{A}
,
\mathsf{H}
and
\mathsf{P}
.

The
\mathsf{HP}
-relation states are modified in the following way.

Definition 14

(
\mathsf{HP}
-Relation States)
.

For two integers
l
and
r
such that
0\leq l+r\leq N
, and two relations

L=\left\{(x_{1},y_{1}),\dots,(x_{l},y_{l})\right\}\in\mathfrak{R}_{l}
and

R=\left\{(x^{\prime}_{1},y^{\prime}_{1}),\dots,(x^{\prime}_{r},y^{\prime}_{r})\right\}\in\mathfrak{R}_{r}
, we define

\displaystyle\ket{\phi_{L,R}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{HP}}}\coloneq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{3d(n-1)}(N-l-r)!}}\sum_{f,\sigma}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\{0,1\}^{l}\\
b^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{r}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\prod_{i=1}^{l}\bra{y_{i}}H_{f}\ket{y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}}\delta_{y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}=\sigma(x_{i})}\prod_{i=1}^{r}\bra{{y^{\prime}}_{i}^{\oplus b^{\prime}_{i}}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y^{\prime}_{i}}\delta_{{y^{\prime}}_{i}^{\oplus b^{\prime}_{i}}=\sigma(x^{\prime}_{i})}\ket{f}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{H}}}\ket{\sigma}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{P}}}\kern 4.62497pt.

where
x^{\oplus 0}=x
and
x^{\oplus 1}=\overline{x}
for any
x\in\{0,1\}^{n}
, and
\delta_{y=x}
is an indicator that
equals
1
iff strings
x
and
y
are identical in every coordinate.

The
\mathsf{HP}
-relation states are orthonormal if we require

L\cup R\in\mathfrak{R}^{\mathrm{DB}}
.

Lemma 12

\left\{\ket{\phi_{L,R}}\right\}_{L,R:L\cup R\in\mathfrak{R}^{\mathrm{DB}}}
forms a set of orthonormal vectors.

Intuitively, the action of
\mathsf{HPO}
is to add the query pair
(x,y)
into the set
L
,
while the action of
\mathsf{HPO}^{\dagger}
is to add the query pair
(x,y)
into the set
R
.

Lemma 13

For two integers
l
and
r
such that
0\leq l+r\leq N
, and two relations

L=\left\{(x_{1},y_{1}),\dots,(x_{l},y_{l})\right\}\in\mathfrak{R}_{l}
and

R=\left\{(x^{\prime}_{1},y^{\prime}_{1}),\dots,(x^{\prime}_{r},y^{\prime}_{r})\right\}\in\mathfrak{R}_{r}
, we have for
x\in\{0,1\}^{n}

\mathsf{HPO}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{AHP}}}\ket{x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\ket{\phi_{L,R}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{HP}}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-l-r}}\sum_{y\in\{0,1\}^{n}}\ket{y}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\otimes\ket{\phi_{L\cup\left\{x,y\right\},R}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{HP}}}\kern 5.0pt.

Similarly, we have for
y\in\{0,1\}^{n}

\mathsf{HPO}^{\dagger}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{AHP}}}\ket{y}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\ket{\phi_{L,R}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{HP}}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-l-r}}\sum_{x\in\{0,1\}^{n}}\ket{x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\otimes\ket{\phi_{L,R\cup\left\{x,y\right\}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{HP}}}\kern 5.0pt.

We provide the proofs of the above two lemmas in
Appendix
0.C
.

5.2
Partial Path-Recording Oracle
W

We first introduce
some relevant notation from
[
22
]
.
For
t\in\mathbb{N}
, the register
\mathsf{R}^{(t)}\coloneq\left(\mathsf{R}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{X}},\mathsf{R}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{Y}}\right)

is defined with the Hilbert space

\mathcal{H}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}^{(t)}}}\coloneq\mathcal{H}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{X}}}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{Y}}}}\coloneq\left(\mathbb{C}^{N}\right)^{\otimes t}\otimes\left(\mathbb{C}^{N}\right)^{\otimes t}\kern 5.0pt.

Note that
\mathsf{R}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{X}}
and
\mathsf{R}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{Y}}
both consist of
t
registers with Hilbert space
\mathbb{C}^{N}
.
For
i\leq t
, let
\mathsf{R}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{X},i}
denote the
i
-th register in
\mathsf{R}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{X}}
,
and
\mathsf{R}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{Y},i}
denote the
i
-th register in
\mathsf{R}^{(t)}_{\mathsf{Y}}
.
The register
\mathsf{R}
is defined with the infinite-dimensional Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}}}\coloneq\bigoplus_{t\geq 0}\mathcal{H}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}^{(t)}}}.

And the register
\mathsf{L}
is defined in the same way.

For integers
l,r\geq 0
,
\Pi_{l,r,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LR}}}

is the projector onto the Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{L}^{(l)}}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}^{(r)}}}
.
For
t\geq 0
,
\Pi_{\leq t,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LR}}}

is the projector onto the Hilbert space

\bigoplus_{l,r\geq 0:l+r\leq t}\mathcal{H}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{L}^{(l)}}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}^{(r)}}}
.
For an operator
M_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LR}}}
,

M_{l,r,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LR}}}\coloneq M_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LR}}}\cdot\Pi_{l,r,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LR}}}
,

M_{\leq t,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LR}}}\coloneq M_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LR}}}\cdot\Pi_{\leq t,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LR}}}
,
and
M_{\leq t,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LR}}}^{\dagger}
is
\left(M_{\leq t,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LR}}}\right)^{\dagger}
. For any unitary
U
, define
U^{\otimes*}\coloneq\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}U^{\otimes t}
.

Definition 15
.

Let
L,R
be such that
L\cup R\in\mathfrak{R}^{\mathrm{DB}}
.
We define operators
W^{L}
and
W^{R}
to be the linear maps
such that for
x\in\{0,1\}^{n}
and
x\notin\mathrm{BDom}\left(L\cup R\right)
,

W^{L}\ket{x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\ket{L}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{L}}}\ket{R}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-2\left|L\cup R\right|}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in\{0,1\}^{n}\\
y\notin\mathrm{BIm}\left(L\cup R\right)\end{subarray}}\ket{y}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\otimes\ket{L\cup\left\{x,y\right\}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{L}}}\otimes\ket{R}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}}}\kern 5.0pt,

and for
y\in\{0,1\}^{n}
and
y\notin\mathrm{BIm}\left(L\cup R\right)
,

W^{R}\ket{y}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\ket{L}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{L}}}\ket{R}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-2\left|L\cup R\right|}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\{0,1\}^{n}\\
x\notin\mathrm{BDom}\left(L\cup R\right)\end{subarray}}\ket{x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\otimes\ket{L}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{L}}}\otimes\ket{R\cup\left\{x,y\right\}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}}}\kern 5.0pt.

The partial path-recording oracle
W
is defined as
W=W^{L}+W^{R,{\dagger}}
.

By the above definition, it is easy to verify that
W^{L}
and
W^{R}
are partial isometries, and that

W^{L}\cdot W^{R}=W^{R,{\dagger}}\cdot W^{L,{\dagger}}=0\kern 5.0pt.

Thus

W^{\dagger}\cdot W=W^{L,{\dagger}}\cdot W^{L}+W^{R}\cdot W^{R,{\dagger}}\kern 5.0pt.

(10)

Therefore
W\cdot W^{\dagger}\cdot W=W
,
which implies
W
is a partial isometry.

Notation 5.4
.

For a partial isometry
G
, let
\Pi^{\mathrm{Dom}\left(G\right)}=G^{\dagger}\cdot G
and
\Pi^{\mathrm{Im}\left(G\right)}=G\cdot G^{\dagger}
denote the orthogonal projectors onto
\mathrm{Dom}\left(G\right)
and
\mathrm{Im}\left(G\right)
.

Up to the partial isometry
\mathsf{Compress}:\mathcal{H}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{P}}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{F}}}\to\mathcal{H}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{L}}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}}}
defined by

\mathsf{Compress}:=\sum_{L\cup R\in\mathfrak{R}^{\mathrm{DB}}}\left(\ket{L}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{L}}}\otimes\ket{R}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}}}\right)\bra{\phi_{L,R}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{PF}}}\kern 5.0pt,

the oracle
W
is an approximate restriction of
\mathsf{HPO}
:

Lemma 14

For
0\leq t<N
,

\left\lVert W_{\leq t}-\mathsf{Compress}\cdot\mathsf{HPO}\cdot\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi^{\mathrm{Dom}\left(W\right)}\cdot\Pi_{\leq t}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\frac{2t}{N-t}\kern 5.0pt,

(11)

\left\lVert(W^{\dagger})_{\leq t}-\mathsf{Compress}\cdot\mathsf{HPO}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi^{\mathrm{Im}\left(W\right)}\cdot\Pi_{\leq t}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\frac{2t}{N-t}\kern 5.0pt.

(12)

Proof.

We will prove
Eq.

11
, and
Eq.

12
follows from a symmetric argument. Denote

X=\mathsf{Compress}\cdot\mathsf{HPO}\cdot\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi^{\mathrm{Dom}\left(W\right)}\cdot\Pi_{\leq t}\kern 5.0pt,

X^{L}=\mathsf{Compress}\cdot\mathsf{HPO}\cdot\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi^{\mathrm{Dom}\left(W^{L}\right)}\cdot\Pi_{\leq t}

and

X^{R}=\Pi_{\leq t}\cdot\Pi^{\mathrm{Im}\left(W^{R}\right)}\cdot\mathsf{Compress}\cdot\mathsf{HPO}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}\kern 5.0pt.

Then by
Eq.

10
,
X=X^{L}+X^{R,{\dagger}}
. To prove
Eq.

11
, it suffices to prove

\left\lVert W^{L}_{\leq t}-X^{L}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\frac{t}{N-t}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\left\lVert(W^{R,{\dagger}})_{\leq t}-X^{R,{\dagger}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\frac{t}{N-t}\kern 5.0pt.

(13)

Next, we prove the first inequality in
Eq.

13
. The other inequality follows from a symmetric argument.
For
L,R
such that
L\cup R\in\mathfrak{R}^{\mathrm{DB}}
and
\left|L\cup R\right|\leq t
,
and
x\in\{0,1\}^{n}
such that
x\notin\mathrm{BDom}\left(L\cup R\right)
, we have

X^{L}\ket{x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\ket{L}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{L}}}\ket{R}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-\left|L\cup R\right|}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in\{0,1\}^{n}\\
y\notin\mathrm{BIm}\left(L\cup R\right)\end{subarray}}\ket{y}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\otimes\ket{L\cup\left\{x,y\right\}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{L}}}\otimes\ket{R}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}}}\kern 5.0pt,

and

W^{L}_{\leq t}\ket{x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\ket{L}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{L}}}\ket{R}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-2\left|L\cup R\right|}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in\{0,1\}^{n}\\
y\notin\mathrm{BIm}\left(L\cup R\right)\end{subarray}}\ket{y}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\otimes\ket{L\cup\left\{x,y\right\}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{L}}}\otimes\ket{R}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}}}\kern 5.0pt.

Then for such
x,L,R
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\left(W^{L}_{\leq t}-X^{L}\right)\ket{x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\ket{L}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{L}}}\ket{R}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}}}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\left\lVert\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-\left|L\cup R\right|}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-2\left|L\cup R\right|}}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in\{0,1\}^{n}\\
y\notin\mathrm{BIm}\left(L\cup R\right)\end{subarray}}\ket{y}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\otimes\ket{L\cup\left\{x,y\right\}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{L}}}\otimes\ket{R}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}}}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\sqrt{\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-\left|L\cup R\right|}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-2\left|L\cup R\right|}}\right)^{2}\left(N-2\left|L\cup R\right|\right)}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle 1-\sqrt{1-\frac{\left|L\cup R\right|}{N-\left|L\cup R\right|}}

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{\left|L\cup R\right|}{N-\left|L\cup R\right|}\kern 4.62497pt.

Note that for other
x,L,R
,

X^{L}\ket{x}\ket{L}\ket{R}=W^{L}\ket{x}\ket{L}\ket{R}=0
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\left\lVert W^{L}_{\leq t}-X^{L}\right\rVert_{\infty}=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,L,R:\\
L\cup R\in\mathfrak{R}^{\mathrm{DB}},\left|L\cup R\right|\leq t\\
x\notin\mathrm{BDom}\left(L\cup R\right)\end{subarray}}\left\lVert\left(W^{L}_{\leq t}-X^{L}\right)\ket{x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\ket{L}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{L}}}\ket{R}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}}}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\frac{t}{N-t}\kern 5.0pt.

This completes the proof.
∎

5.3
Path-Recording Oracle
V

Definition 16
.

Let
L,R\in\mathfrak{R}
.
We define operators
V^{L}
and
V^{R}
to be the linear maps
such that for
x\in\{0,1\}^{n}
,

V^{L}\ket{x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\ket{L}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{L}}}\ket{R}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-\left|\mathrm{BIm}\left(L\cup R\right)\right|}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in\{0,1\}^{n}\\
y\notin\mathrm{BIm}\left(L\cup R\right)\end{subarray}}\ket{y}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\otimes\ket{L\cup\left\{x,y\right\}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{L}}}\otimes\ket{R}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}}}\,,

and for
y\in\{0,1\}^{n}

V^{R}\ket{y}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\ket{L}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{L}}}\ket{R}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-\left|\mathrm{BDom}\left(L\cup R\right)\right|}}\!\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\{0,1\}^{n}\\
x\notin\mathrm{BDom}\left(L\cup R\right)\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\ket{x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\otimes\ket{L}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{L}}}\otimes\ket{R\cup\left\{x,y\right\}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}}}\,.

The path-recording oracle
V
is defined to be

V=V^{L}\cdot(\mathds{1}-V^{R}\cdot V^{R,{\dagger}})+(\mathds{1}-V^{L}\cdot V^{L,{\dagger}})\cdot V^{R,{\dagger}}\kern 5.0pt.

(14)

By the above definition, it is easy to verify that
V^{L}
and
V^{R}
are partial isometries, and that
V^{L}(V^{R})
differs from
W^{L}(W^{R})
by only a projection, i.e.,

V^{L}\cdot\Pi^{\mathrm{Dom}\left(W^{L}\right)}=W^{L}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\Pi^{\mathrm{Im}\left(W^{R}\right)}\cdot V^{R}=W^{R}\kern 5.0pt.

(15)

Also, we have

W^{L}\cdot V^{R}=W^{R}\cdot V^{L}=0\kern 5.0pt.

(16)

Claim 5.5
.

V
is a partial isometry.

Proof.

We first show that
V^{L}\cdot(\mathds{1}-V^{R}\cdot V^{R,{\dagger}})
is a partial isometry, which is true if and only if
(\mathds{1}-V^{R}\cdot V^{R,{\dagger}})\cdot V^{L,{\dagger}}\cdot V^{L}\cdot(\mathds{1}-V^{R}\cdot V^{R,{\dagger}})
is a projector. It suffices to show that
V^{L,{\dagger}}\cdot V^{L}
and
V^{R}\cdot V^{R,{\dagger}}
commute. By the definition of
V^{L}
,
V^{L,{\dagger}}\cdot V^{L}=\mathds{1}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\otimes\Pi_{\leq N-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LR}}}
. Since
V^{R}\cdot V^{R,{\dagger}}
takes states in
\Pi_{\leq i+1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LR}}}
to
\Pi_{\leq i+1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LR}}}
(for
0\leq i\leq N-1
), it commutes with

\mathds{1}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\otimes\Pi_{\leq N-1,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LR}}}
.
Moreover, we know that
(\mathds{1}-V^{L}\cdot V^{L,{\dagger}})\cdot V^{R,{\dagger}}
is also a partial isometry by a similar argument.

To show that
V
is a partial isometry, we then show that the domains of
V^{L}\cdot(\mathds{1}-V^{R}\cdot V^{R,{\dagger}})
and
(\mathds{1}-V^{L}\cdot V^{L,{\dagger}})\cdot V^{R,{\dagger}}
are orthogonal.
Indeed, these domains lie within the subspaces
\mathds{1}-\Pi^{\mathrm{Im}\left(V^{R}\right)}
and
\Pi^{\mathrm{Im}\left(V^{R}\right)}
, respectively, and are therefore orthogonal.
∎

We formalize the fact that
W
is a restriction of
V
in the following lemma.

Lemma 15

W=V\cdot\Pi^{\mathrm{Dom}\left(W\right)}\kern 5.0pt,

(17)

W^{\dagger}=V^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi^{\mathrm{Im}\left(W\right)}\kern 5.0pt.

(18)

Proof.

To prove
Eq.

17
, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle W^{L}

\displaystyle=V\cdot\Pi^{\mathrm{Dom}\left(W^{L}\right)}\kern 5.0pt,

(19)

\displaystyle W^{R,{\dagger}}

\displaystyle=V\cdot\Pi^{\mathrm{Im}\left(W^{R}\right)}\kern 5.0pt.

(20)

By
Eq.

10
,
Eq.

17
can be obtained by summing these two equations.

We now prove
Eq.

19
. By
Eq.

14
, we have

V\cdot\Pi^{\mathrm{Dom}\left(W^{L}\right)}=\left(V^{L}\cdot(\mathds{1}-V^{R}\cdot V^{R,{\dagger}})+(\mathds{1}-V^{L}\cdot V^{L,{\dagger}})\cdot V^{R,{\dagger}}\right)\cdot\Pi^{\mathrm{Dom}\left(W^{L}\right)}\,.

By
Eq.

16
, we have

V^{R,{\dagger}}\cdot\Pi^{\mathrm{Dom}\left(W^{L}\right)}=V^{R,{\dagger}}\cdot W^{L,{\dagger}}\cdot W^{L}=0\kern 5.0pt.

Thus

V\cdot\Pi^{\mathrm{Dom}\left(W^{L}\right)}=V^{L}\cdot\Pi^{\mathrm{Dom}\left(W^{L}\right)}=W^{L}\kern 5.0pt,

where the second equality follows from
Eq.

15
.

It remains to prove
Eq.

20
. By
Eq.

14
, we have

V\cdot\Pi^{\mathrm{Im}\left(W^{R}\right)}=\left(V^{L}\cdot(\mathds{1}-V^{R}\cdot V^{R,{\dagger}})+(\mathds{1}-V^{L}\cdot V^{L,{\dagger}})\cdot V^{R,{\dagger}}\right)\cdot\Pi^{\mathrm{Im}\left(W^{R}\right)}\,.

By the definition of
V^{R}
and
W^{R}
, we have that
\mathrm{Im}(W^{R})\subseteq\mathrm{Im}(V^{R})
. Thus

(\mathds{1}-V^{R}\cdot V^{R,{\dagger}})\Pi^{\mathrm{Im}\left(W^{R}\right)}=\Pi^{\mathrm{Im}\left(W^{R}\right)}-\Pi^{\mathrm{Im}\left(V^{R}\right)}\Pi^{\mathrm{Im}\left(W^{R}\right)}=0\kern 5.0pt.

At last, by
Eq.

15
and
Eq.

16
, we have

(\mathds{1}-V^{L}\cdot V^{L,{\dagger}})\cdot V^{R,{\dagger}}\cdot\Pi^{\mathrm{Im}\left(W^{R}\right)}=(\mathds{1}-V^{L}\cdot V^{L,{\dagger}})W^{R,{\dagger}}=W^{R,{\dagger}}\kern 5.0pt.

The proof of
Eq.

18
follows from a similar argument.
∎

Two-sided Unitary Invariance

The modified path-recording oracle
V
also admits an approximate two-sided unitary invariance as in
[
22
, Section 8.3]
. We state it below and provide the proof in
Appendix
0.D
.

Definition 17
.

For any two
n
-qubit unitaries
C
and
D
, define

Q[C,D]\coloneq(C\otimes D^{T})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{L}}}^{\otimes*}\otimes(\overline{C}\otimes D^{\dagger})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}}}^{\otimes*}\kern 5.0pt.

Lemma 16

For any two
n
-qubit unitaries
C
and
D
, and for an integer
0\leq t\leq N-1
,

\left\lVert D_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\cdot V_{\leq t}\cdot C_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\cdot Q[C,D]_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LR}}}-Q[C,D]_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LR}}}\cdot V_{\leq t}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq 32\sqrt{\frac{t(t+1)}{N}}\kern 5.0pt,

\left\lVert C^{\dagger}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\cdot(V^{\dagger})_{\leq t}\cdot D^{\dagger}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\cdot Q[C,D]_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LR}}}-Q[C,D]_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LR}}}\cdot(V^{\dagger})_{\leq t}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq 32\sqrt{\frac{t(t+1)}{N}}\kern 5.0pt.

5.4
Main Proof

We are now prepared to prove
Theorem
5.1
,
which demonstrates the strong security
of our construction
\mathsf{HP}_{n,2T+1}
.
We first show that
our construction is indistinguishable from
the path-recording oracle
V
.

Lemma 17

(
\mathsf{HP}_{n,2T+1}
is indistinguishable from
V
)

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a
t
-query oracle adversary capable of
performing both forward and inverse queries. Let

\mathsf{HP}_{n,2T+1}
be defined as in
Definition
8
with
T=30n
and
d=5n
, and
V
be
defined as in
Definition
16
. Then

\displaystyle\mathrm{TD}\left(\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{O}\leftarrow\mathsf{HP}_{n,2T+1}\end{subarray}}\!\left[\ket{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}}_{t}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}}_{t}}_{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{AB}}}}\right],\mathrm{Tr}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LR}}}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}_{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLR}}}}\right)\right)\leq\frac{t(11t+20)}{N^{1/8}}\kern 5.0pt.

Then, we show the Haar random unitary is indistinguishable from
V
as well:

Lemma 18

(
V
is indistinguishable from Haar random unitaries)

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a
t
-query oracle adversary capable of
performing both forward and inverse queries. Then

\mathrm{TD}\left(\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{O}\leftarrow\mu\end{subarray}}\!\left[\ket{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}}_{t}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}}_{t}}_{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{AB}}}}\right],\mathrm{Tr}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LR}}}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}_{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLR}}}}\right)\right)\leq\frac{t(11t+20)}{N^{1/8}}\kern 5.0pt.

In the following sections, we provide the details of the main lemma
Lemma
17
. The proof is
structured as follows: first, we demonstrate that
V

is indistinguishable from the twirled partial path-recording
oracle
W
; then, we show that the twirled
W
is indistinguishable
from the twirled purified
\mathsf{HPO}
oracle (
Definition
22
).

Lemma
18
follows from a similar argument.
Finally, by applying the triangle inequality to

Lemma
17
and
Lemma
18
, we conclude the proof of

Theorem
5.1
at the end of this section.

V
Is Indistinguishable from
W

Our goal here is to establish that
even if the adversary can make queries from both directions,
the path-recording oracle
V
is
indistinguishable from
D\cdot W\cdot C
for
C,D\leftarrow\mathcal{D}
,
as long as
\mathcal{D}
is one of the following two distributions:

Definition 18
.

We define two distributions of unitaries:

•

\mathcal{D}_{1}
: sample two independent unitary
operators
C
and
D
from Haar measure on

\mathrm{U}\!\left(N\right)
, and output
C
and
D
,

•

\mathcal{D}_{2}
: sample independent
C^{\prime}
and
D
from

\mathsf{HP}_{n,T}
with
T=30n
and
d=5n
, and a random
permutation matrix
P
. Output
C=P\cdot C^{\prime}
and
D
.

We first introduce some notation.
Let

\Pi^{\mathrm{DB}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LR}}}\coloneq\sum_{L,R:L\cup R\in\mathfrak{R}^{\mathrm{DB}}}\ket{L}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{L}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{L}}}\otimes\ket{R}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{R}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}}}\kern 5.0pt,\kern 5.0pt\Pi^{\mathfrak{R}^{2}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LR}}}\coloneq\sum_{L,R\in\mathfrak{R}}\ket{L}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{L}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{L}}}\otimes\ket{R}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{R}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}}}\kern 5.0pt.

Definition 19

(Controlled
C
,
D
and
Q
)
.

Define the following operators:

\displaystyle\mathsf{cC}

\displaystyle\coloneq\int_{C}C_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\otimes\ket{C}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{C}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{C}}}\quad,\qquad\mathsf{cD}\coloneq\int_{D}D_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\otimes\ket{D}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{D}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{D}}}\quad,

\displaystyle\mathsf{cQ}

\displaystyle\coloneq\int_{C,D}Q[C,D]_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LR}}}\otimes\ket{C}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{C}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{C}}}\otimes\ket{D}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{D}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{D}}}\quad,

where
Q[C,D]\coloneq(C\otimes D^{T})^{\otimes*}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{L}}}\otimes(\overline{C}\otimes D^{\dagger})^{\otimes*}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}}}
.

We then define the quantum states after querying the twirled-
W
and
V
.

Definition 20

(Purification of Twirled-
W
)
.

We define the adversary state
\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}
after
the
i
-th query to twirled-
W
as follows:

•

For
i=0
,

\ket{\mathcal{A}_{0}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\coloneq\ket{0^{n}0^{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{AB}}}\ket{\left\{\right\}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{L}}}\ket{\left\{\right\}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}}}\ket{\mathsf{init}(\mathcal{D})}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{CD}}}

where

\ket{\mathsf{init}(\mathcal{D})}\coloneq\int_{C,D}\sqrt{d\mu_{\mathcal{D}}(C)d\mu_{\mathcal{D}}(D)}\ket{C}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{C}}}\ket{D}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{D}}}

is the initial purification on registers
\mathsf{C,D}
set up for

C,D\leftarrow\mathcal{D}
; and
\mu_{\mathcal{D}}(\cdot)
denotes the probability measure
of unitaries sampled from the distribution
\mathcal{D}
.

•

For
1\leq i\leq t
,

\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\coloneq\left(\left(1-b_{i}\right)\cdot\left(\mathsf{cD}\cdot W\cdot\mathsf{cC}\right)+b_{i}\cdot\left(\mathsf{cD}\cdot W\cdot\mathsf{cC}\right)^{\dagger}\right)\cdot A_{i}\cdot\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}

where
b_{i}\in\left\{0,1\right\}
indicates that the adversary makes a
forward/inverse query in the
i
-th step.

Definition 21

(Purification of
V
)
.

Define the adversary state
\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i}^{V}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLR}}}
after the
i
-th
query to oracle
V
as follows:

•

For
i=0
,

\ket{\mathcal{A}_{0}^{V}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLR}}}\coloneq\ket{0^{n}0^{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{AB}}}\ket{\left\{\right\}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{L}}}\ket{\left\{\right\}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{R}}}

•

For
1\leq i\leq t
,

\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i}^{V}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLR}}}\coloneq\left(\left(1-b_{i}\right)\cdot V+b_{i}\cdot V^{\dagger}\right)\cdot A_{i}\cdot\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}^{V}}

where
b_{i}\in\left\{0,1\right\}
indicates that the adversary makes a
forward/inverse query in the
i
-th step.

Fact 5.6

(Norm of the purified states)
.

For any
t\geq 0
,
\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}
and
\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}
both have
a norm at most
1
, since
W
and
V
are partial isometries,
meaning that applying
W,W^{\dagger},V
or
V^{\dagger}
is equivalent to a
projection followed by a unitary operation.

Fact 5.7

(Spaces containing purified states)
.

By definition, for any
t\geq 0
,
\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}
lies in the image of
\Pi_{\leq t}^{\mathrm{DB}}
, and
\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}
lies in the image of
\Pi_{\leq t}^{\mathfrak{R}^{2}}
.

We now state the following claim, which shows that the state produced by twirled-
W
is almost the same as that from
V
, up to a local unitary transformation acting only on the registers
\mathsf{LRCD}
.
The proof of this claim follows a similar argument to that of
[
22
, Claim 18]
, with the full details provided in
Appendix
0.E
.

Claim 5.8
.

For any integer
t\geq 0
, and
\mathcal{D}\in\left\{\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2}\right\}
as defined in
Definition
18

\mathrm{Re}\left[\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\cdot\mathsf{cQ}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LRCD}}}\cdot\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLR}}}\ket{\mathsf{init}(\mathcal{D})}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{CD}}}\right)\right]\geq 1-\frac{38t^{2}}{N^{1/4}}\kern 5.0pt.

Now, we are ready to prove the indistinguishability between oracles
V
and twirled
W
.

Lemma 19

For any
0\leq t<N
and
\mathcal{D}\in\left\{\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2}\right\}
as defined in
Definition
18
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{TD}{\left(\mathrm{Tr}{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\right),\mathrm{Tr}{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLR}}}\right)\right)\leq\frac{9t}{N^{1/8}}\kern 5.0pt.}

Proof.

Using the fact that
\left\lVert\ket{u}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{u}-\ket{v}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{v}\right\rVert_{1}\leq 2\left\lVert\ket{u}-\ket{v}\right\rVert_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle~\mathrm{TD}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}\hskip-3.6999pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}},\mathsf{cQ}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LRCD}}}\cdot\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}\hskip-3.6999pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLR}}}\otimes\ket{\mathsf{init}(\mathcal{D})}\hskip-3.6999pt\bra{\mathsf{init}(\mathcal{D})}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{CD}}}\right)\cdot\mathsf{cQ}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LRCD}}}^{\dagger}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\left\lVert\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLR}}}-\mathsf{cQ}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LRCD}}}\cdot\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLR}}}\otimes\ket{\mathsf{init}(\mathcal{D})_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{CD}}}}\right)\right\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\braket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}\mid\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}+\braket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}\mid\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}-2\mathrm{Re}\left[\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\cdot\mathsf{cQ}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LRCD}}}\cdot\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLR}}}\ket{\mathsf{init}(\mathcal{D})}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{CD}}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~2-2\cdot\left(1-\frac{38t^{2}}{N^{1/4}}\right)=\frac{76t^{2}}{N^{1/4}}\kern 4.62497pt.

Since unitary
\mathsf{cQ}
only acts on registers
\mathsf{L,R,C,D}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{TD}{\left(\mathrm{Tr}{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}\hskip-3.6999pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\right),\mathrm{Tr}{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}\hskip-3.6999pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLR}}}\right)\right)}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\mathrm{TD}\biggl(\mathrm{Tr}{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}\hskip-3.6999pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\right),

\displaystyle\hskip 36.99976pt\mathrm{Tr}{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}}\left(\mathsf{cQ}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LRCD}}}\cdot\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}\hskip-3.6999pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLR}}}\otimes\ket{\mathsf{init}(\mathcal{D})}\hskip-3.6999pt\bra{\mathsf{init}(\mathcal{D})}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{CD}}}\right)\cdot\mathsf{cQ}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LRCD}}}^{\dagger}\right)\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\mathrm{TD}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}\hskip-3.6999pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}},\mathsf{cQ}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LRCD}}}\cdot\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}\hskip-3.6999pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLR}}}\otimes\ket{\mathsf{init}(\mathcal{D})}\hskip-3.6999pt\bra{\mathsf{init}(\mathcal{D})}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{CD}}}\right)\cdot\mathsf{cQ}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LRCD}}}^{\dagger}\right)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{9t}{N^{1/8}}\kern 4.62497pt.\qed

Claim
5.8
also gives a bound
on the norm of
\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}
.

Lemma 20

For any
0\leq t<N
and
\mathcal{D}\in\left\{\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2}\right\}
as defined in
Definition
18
, we have

\left\lVert\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\right\rVert_{2}\geq 1-\frac{38t^{2}}{N^{1/4}}\kern 5.0pt.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\right\rVert^{2}_{2}=

\displaystyle~\langle\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}|\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\langle\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}|\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}\rangle\cdot\langle\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}|\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\langle\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}|\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}\rangle\cdot\left(\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}\bra{\mathsf{init}(\mathcal{D})}\right)\mathsf{cQ}^{\dagger}\cdot\mathsf{cQ}\cdot\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}\ket{\mathsf{init}(\mathcal{D})}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\left|\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\cdot\mathsf{cQ}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LRCD}}}\cdot\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLR}}}\ket{\mathsf{init}(\mathcal{D})}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{CD}}}\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\mathrm{Re}\left[\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\cdot\mathsf{cQ}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{LRCD}}}\cdot\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLR}}}\ket{\mathsf{init}(\mathcal{D})}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{CD}}}\right)\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\left(1-\frac{38t^{2}}{N^{1/4}}\right)^{2}\kern 5.0pt,

where the first inequality uses the fact that

\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}
has norm at most
1
,
the second one is from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,
and the last one is from
Claim
5.8
.
∎

W
Is Indistinguishable from
\mathsf{HPO}

In this section, we show that after twirling,
adversaries cannot distinguish the
\mathsf{HPO}
oracle from
W

(
Lemma
21
).
We first define the purification
of twirled-
\mathsf{HPO}
oracle and then connect it with
twirled-
W
(
Definition
20
) via some projection.

Definition 22

(Purification of twirled-
\mathsf{HPO}
)
.

Define the adversary state
\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\mathsf{HPO},\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABHPCD}}}
after
the
i
-th query to twirled-
\mathsf{HPO}
as follows:

•

For
i=0
,

\displaystyle\ket{\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\mathsf{HPO},\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABHPCD}}}\coloneq\ket{0^{n}0^{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{AB}}}\ket{+_{f}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{H}}}\ket{+_{\sigma}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{P}}}\ket{\mathsf{init}(\mathcal{D})}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{CD}}}

where
\ket{+_{f}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{H}}}
and
\ket{+_{\sigma}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{P}}}
are uniform superposition over
all functions and permutations, respectively, and

\ket{\mathsf{init}(\mathcal{D})}\coloneq\int_{C,D}\sqrt{d\mu_{\mathcal{D}}(C)d\mu_{\mathcal{D}}(D)}\ket{C}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{C}}}\ket{D}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{D}}}

is the initial purification on registers
\mathsf{C,D}
set up for

C,D\leftarrow\mathcal{D}
; and
\mu_{\mathcal{D}}(\cdot)
denotes the probability measure
of unitaries sampled from the distribution
\mathcal{D}
.

•

For
1\leq i\leq t
,

\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\mathsf{HPO},\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABHPCD}}}\coloneq\left(\left(1-b_{i}\right)\cdot\left(\mathsf{cD}\cdot\mathsf{HPO}\cdot\mathsf{cC}\right)+b_{i}\cdot\left(\mathsf{cD}\cdot\mathsf{HPO}\cdot\mathsf{cC}\right)^{\dagger}\right)\cdot A_{i}\cdot\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}^{\mathsf{HPO},\mathcal{D}}}

where
b_{i}\in\left\{0,1\right\}
indicates that the adversary makes a
forward/inverse query in the
i
-th step.

Definition 23
.

Define the projectors

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathrm{Dom}\left(W\right)}

\displaystyle\coloneq\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi^{\mathrm{Dom}\left(W\right)}\cdot\mathsf{Compress}\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathrm{Im}\left(W\right)}

\displaystyle\coloneq\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi^{\mathrm{Im}\left(W\right)}\cdot\mathsf{Compress}\kern 5.0pt.

Definition 24

(Purification of twirled-projected-
\mathsf{HPO}
)
.

Define the adversary state
\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABHPCD}}}
after
the
i
-th query to twirled-
\mathsf{HPO}
with projection:

•

For
i=0
,
\ket{\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}\coloneq\ket{\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\mathsf{HPO},\mathcal{D}}}

•

For
1\leq i\leq t
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}\coloneq&\bigg(\left(1-b_{i}\right)\cdot\left(\mathsf{cD}\cdot\mathsf{HPO}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathrm{Dom}\left(W\right)}\cdot\mathsf{cC}\right)+\\
&\kern 5.0ptb_{i}\cdot\left(\mathsf{cD}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathrm{Im}\left(W\right)}\cdot\mathsf{HPO}\cdot\mathsf{cC}\right)^{\dagger}\bigg)\cdot A_{i}\cdot\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}\end{split}

where
b_{i}\in\left\{0,1\right\}
indicates that the adversary makes a
forward/backward query in the
i
-th step.

Claim 5.9
.

For all integers
0\leq t\leq N/2
,

\left\lVert\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}-\mathsf{Compress}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{HP}}}\cdot\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\frac{2t(t+1)}{N}\kern 5.0pt.

Proof by induction.

First, we check that the claim is true for the base case
t=0
:

\displaystyle\mathsf{Compress}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{HP}}}\cdot\ket{\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Compress}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{HP}}}\cdot\ket{\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\mathsf{HPO},\mathcal{D}}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Compress}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{HP}}}\cdot\left(\ket{0^{n}0^{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{AB}}}\ket{+_{f}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{H}}}\ket{+_{\sigma}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{P}}}\ket{\mathsf{init}(\mathcal{D})}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{CD}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\ket{0^{n}0^{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{AB}}}\ket{\left\{\right\}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{H}}}\ket{\left\{\right\}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{P}}}\ket{\mathsf{init}(\mathcal{D})}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{CD}}}=\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}

Next, assuming the claim for case
(i-1)
, we will show the case
i
.
W.l.o.g, we assume
b_{i}=0
, and then

\displaystyle\left\lVert\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}-\mathsf{Compress}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{HP}}}\cdot\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\,=\left\lVert\mathsf{cD}\cdot W\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{i}\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}-\mathsf{Compress}\cdot\mathsf{cD}\cdot\mathsf{HPO}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathrm{Dom}\left(W\right)}\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{i}\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}\right\rVert_{2}\,.

Note that
\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}
lies in the image of
\Pi_{\leq i-1}
.
Thus,
\Pi_{\leq i-1}\cdot\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}=\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}-\mathsf{Compress}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{HP}}}\cdot\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathsf{cD}\cdot W\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{i}\cdot\Pi_{\leq i-1}\cdot\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\left.-~\mathsf{Compress}\cdot\mathsf{cD}\cdot\mathsf{HPO}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathrm{Dom}\left(W\right)}\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{i}\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathsf{cD}\cdot W_{\leq i-1}\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{i}\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\left.-~\mathsf{Compress}\cdot\mathsf{cD}\cdot\mathsf{HPO}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathrm{Dom}\left(W\right)}\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{i}\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathsf{cD}\cdot\mathsf{Compress}\cdot\mathsf{HPO}\cdot\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi^{\mathrm{Dom}\left(W\right)}\cdot\Pi_{\leq i-1}\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{i}\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad\quad\quad-\mathsf{Compress}\cdot\mathsf{cD}\cdot\mathsf{HPO}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathrm{Dom}\left(W\right)}\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{i}\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}\right\rVert_{2}+\frac{2i-2}{N-i+1}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathsf{Compress}\cdot\mathsf{cD}\cdot\mathsf{HPO}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathrm{Dom}\left(W\right)}\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{i}\cdot\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}\cdot\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad\quad\quad-~\mathsf{Compress}\cdot\mathsf{cD}\cdot\mathsf{HPO}\cdot\widetilde{\Pi}^{\mathrm{Dom}\left(W\right)}\cdot\mathsf{cC}\cdot A_{i}\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}\right\rVert_{2}+\frac{2i-2}{N-i+1}

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\left\lVert\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}-\mathsf{Compress}\cdot\ket{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}\right\rVert_{2}+\frac{4i}{N}

\displaystyle{\leq}~

\displaystyle\frac{2i(i-1)}{N}+\frac{4i}{N}\quad=\frac{2i(i+1)}{N}\kern 5.0pt,

where the first inequality is by
Lemma

14
and the last one is by induction.
∎

We are ready to prove the indistinguishability between
oracles
W
and
\mathsf{HPO}
:

Lemma 21

For all integers
0\leq t\leq N
,
and
\mathcal{D}\in\left\{\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2}\right\}
as defined in
Definition
18

\displaystyle\mathrm{TD}\left(\mathrm{Tr}{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\mathsf{HPO},\mathcal{D}}}\hskip-3.6999pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\mathsf{HPO},\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\right),\mathrm{Tr}{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}\hskip-3.6999pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\right)\right)\leq\frac{11t(t+1)}{N^{1/8}}\kern 4.62497pt.

Proof.

Recall the state
\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABHPCD}}}
defined in
Definition
24
.
By the triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{TD}\left(\mathrm{Tr}{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\mathsf{HPO},\mathcal{D}}}\hskip-3.6999pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\mathsf{HPO},\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\right),\mathrm{Tr}{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}\hskip-3.6999pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathrm{TD}\left(\mathrm{Tr}{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\mathsf{HPO},\mathcal{D}}}\hskip-3.6999pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\mathsf{HPO},\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\right),\mathrm{Tr}{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}\hskip-3.6999pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABHPCD}}}\right)\right)}_{(\star)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+~\underbrace{\mathrm{TD}\left(\mathrm{Tr}{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}\hskip-3.6999pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABHPCD}}}\right),\mathrm{Tr}{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}\hskip-3.6999pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\right)\right)}_{(*)}\kern 4.62497pt.

It suffices to show that

(\star)\leq~\frac{9t(t+1)}{N^{1/8}}\quad\text{and}\quad(*)\leq~\frac{2t(t+1)}{N}\kern 5.0pt.

We first bound term
(\star)
.
Since
\mathsf{HPO}
and
\mathsf{Compress}
are isometries,
we have
\left\lVert\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\mathsf{HPO},\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABHPCD}}}\right\rVert_{2}=1

and
\left\lVert\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABHPCD}}}\right\rVert_{2}\leq 1
.
Since

\left\lVert\ket{u}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{u}-\ket{v}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{v}\right\rVert_{1}\leq 2\left\lVert\ket{u}-\ket{v}\right\rVert_{2}

for
\left\lVert u\right\rVert_{2}\leq 1
and
\left\lVert v\right\rVert_{2}\leq 1
,
we have

\displaystyle(\star)\leq

\displaystyle~\mathrm{TD}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\mathsf{HPO},\mathcal{D}}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\mathsf{HPO},\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}},\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABHPCD}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\left\lVert\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\mathsf{HPO},\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}-\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABHPCD}}}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~t\cdot\sqrt{1-\left\lVert\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABHPCD}}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}}\kern 5.0pt,

(21)

where the last inequality is from the gentle measurement lemma in

Lemma
2
.
Notice that, since
\mathsf{Compress}
is an isometry, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABHPCD}}}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=~\left\lVert\mathsf{Compress}\cdot\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABHPCD}}}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\geq~\left\lVert\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\right\rVert_{2}-\frac{2t(t+1)}{N}

\displaystyle\geq~1-\frac{38t^{2}}{N^{1/4}}-\frac{2t(t+1)}{N}

\displaystyle\geq~1-\frac{40t(t+1)}{N^{1/4}}\kern 5.0pt,

(22)

where the first inequality is from the triangle inequality
and
Claim
5.9
,
and the second one is from
Lemma
20
.
Combining
Eqs.

21
and
22
,
we have

(\star)\leq t\cdot\sqrt{1-\left(1-\frac{40t(t+1)}{N^{1/4}}\right)^{2}}\leq t\cdot\sqrt{\frac{80t(t+1)}{N^{1/4}}}\leq\frac{9t(t+1)}{N^{1/8}}\kern 5.0pt.

As for term
(*)
, note that
\mathsf{Compress}
acts on environment registers.
We have

\displaystyle(*)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{TD}\!\left(\mathrm{Tr}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}\!\left[\mathsf{Compress}\cdot\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABHPCD}}}\cdot\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger}\right],\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.\mathrm{Tr}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}\!\left[\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{TD}\!\left(\mathsf{Compress}\cdot\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABHPCD}}}\!\!\!\cdot\mathsf{Compress}^{\dagger},\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\mathsf{Compress}\cdot\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\widetilde{\mathsf{HPO}},\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABHPCD}}}-\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2t(t+1)}{N}\kern 5.0pt,

where the second inequality is from the fact that

\left\lVert\ket{u}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{u}-\ket{v}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{v}\right\rVert_{1}\leq 2\left\lVert\ket{u}-\ket{v}\right\rVert_{2}

for
\left\lVert u\right\rVert_{2}\leq 1
and
\left\lVert v\right\rVert_{2}\leq 1
,
and the last one is from
Claim
5.9
.
∎

The Strong Security of
\mathsf{HPO}

Now, we complete the main proof of
Theorem
5.1
by mainly
establishing
Lemma
17
and
Lemma
18

that both our construction and Haar distribution are indistinguishable from
V
.

Proof of
Lemma
17
.

Consider
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{2}
defined in
Definition
18
.
First, due to the perfect indistinguishability between the standard oracle
and its purified version (
Fact
4.3
),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\mathsf{HPO},\mathcal{D}}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\mathsf{HPO},\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABHPCD}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}\begin{subarray}{c}H_{f}P_{\sigma}\\
C,D\leftarrow\mathcal{D}\end{subarray}\end{subarray}}\!\left[\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{DH_{f}P_{\sigma}C}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{DH_{f}P_{\sigma}C}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{AB}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{O}\leftarrow\mathsf{HP}_{n,2T+1}\end{subarray}}\!\left[\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\mathcal{O}}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\mathcal{O}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{AB}}}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

And, from
Lemma
19
, we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{TD}{\left(\mathrm{Tr}{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\right),\mathrm{Tr}{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLR}}}\right)\right)\leq\frac{9t}{N^{1/8}}\kern 5.0pt.}

Then, according to
Lemma
21
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{TD}\left(\mathrm{Tr}{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\mathsf{HPO},\mathcal{D}}}\hskip-3.6999pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\mathsf{HPO},\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\right),\mathrm{Tr}{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}\hskip-3.6999pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\right)\right)\leq\frac{11t(t+1)}{N^{1/8}}\kern 4.62497pt.

Thus, via the triangle inequality, we show:

\displaystyle\mathrm{TD}\left(\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{O}\leftarrow\mathsf{HP}_{n,2T+1}\end{subarray}}\!\left[\ket{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}}_{t}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{O}}_{t}}_{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{AB}}}}\right],\mathrm{Tr}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}_{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLR}}}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{TD}\left(\mathrm{Tr}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\mathsf{HPO},\mathcal{D}}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\mathsf{HPO},\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABHPCD}}}\right),\mathrm{Tr}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}_{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLR}}}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{TD}{\left(\mathrm{Tr}{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\right),\mathrm{Tr}{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{V}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLR}}}\right)\right)}

\displaystyle\kern 5.0pt\kern 5.0pt+\mathrm{TD}\left(\mathrm{Tr}{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\mathsf{HPO},\mathcal{D}}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\mathsf{HPO},\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\right),\mathrm{Tr}{{}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{-AB}}}}\left(\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{W,\mathcal{D}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABLRCD}}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{11t^{2}+20t}{N^{1/8}}\kern 5.0pt.\qed

The argument above also proves
Lemma
18
by considering

\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{1}
as defined in
Definition
18
.
Therefore, combining
Lemma
17
and
Lemma
18
yields
Theorem
5.1
.
We can finally conclude that our construction, based on parallel Kac’s walk, satisfies strong security against adversaries making inverse queries.

Theorem 5.10

By replacing random functions and random permutations with
their post-quantum secure pseudorandom counterparts in the sampling procedure of
\widehat{\mathsf{HP}}_{n,2T+1}
where
T=30n
and
d=5n
, we obtain a strong
PRU
.

Appendix 0.A
Proof of
Lemma
5

Lemma
5
.

\left\{\ket{\phi_{R}}\right\}_{R\in\mathfrak{R}^{\mathrm{DB}}}
forms a set of orthogonal vectors.

Before proving this lemma, we show some properties of
H_{f}
:

Lemma 62

For any
x\in\{0,1\}^{n}
,

1.

\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\end{subarray}}\!\left[\bra{x}H_{f}\ket{x}\right]=\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\end{subarray}}\!\left[\bra{x}H_{f}\ket{\bar{x}}\right]=0
;

2.

\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\end{subarray}}\!\left[\overline{\bra{x}H_{f}\ket{x}}\bra{x}H_{f}\ket{\bar{x}}\right]=0
;

3.

\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\end{subarray}}\!\left[\overline{\bra{x}H_{f}\ket{x}}\bra{\bar{x}}H_{f}\ket{\bar{x}}\right]=\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\end{subarray}}\!\left[\overline{\bra{x}H_{f}\ket{\bar{x}}}\bra{\bar{x}}H_{f}\ket{x}\right]=0.

Proof.

By
Eq.

8
, if
x=0y
for some
y\in\{0,1\}^{n-1}
, then

1.

Note that
\bra{x}H_{f}\ket{x}=\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\alpha_{y}}\cos\theta_{y}
and
\bra{x}H_{f}\ket{\bar{x}}=-\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\beta_{y}}\sin\theta_{y}
. Item 1 holds, since
\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\end{subarray}}\!\left[\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\alpha_{y}}\right]=\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\end{subarray}}\!\left[\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\beta_{y}}\right]=0

2.

Note that
\overline{\bra{x}H_{f}\ket{x}}\bra{x}H_{f}\ket{\bar{x}}=-\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}(\beta_{y}-\alpha_{y})}\sin\theta_{y}\cos\theta_{y}.
Item 2 holds, since
\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\end{subarray}}\!\left[\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}(\beta_{y}-\alpha_{y})}\right]=0
.

3.

Note that
\overline{\bra{x}H_{f}\ket{x}}\bra{\bar{x}}H_{f}\ket{\bar{x}}=\mathrm{e}^{-2\mathrm{i}\alpha_{y}}\cos^{2}\theta_{y}
and
\overline{\bra{x}H_{f}\ket{\bar{x}}}\bra{\bar{x}}H_{f}\ket{x}=-\mathrm{e}^{-2\mathrm{i}\beta_{y}}\sin^{2}\theta_{y}.

Since
\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\end{subarray}}\!\left[\mathrm{e}^{-2\mathrm{i}\alpha_{y}}\right]=\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\end{subarray}}\!\left[\mathrm{e}^{-2\mathrm{i}\beta_{y}}\right]=0
, item 3 holds.

The case when
x=1y
can be argued similarly.
∎

Proof of
Lemma
5
.

Consider two relations
R,S\in\mathfrak{R}^{\mathrm{DB}}
, where
R=\{(x_{1},y_{1}),\dots,

(x_{\left|R\right|},y_{\left|R\right|})\}
and
S=\left\{(x^{\prime}_{1},y^{\prime}_{1}),\dots,(x^{\prime}_{\left|S\right|},y^{\prime}_{\left|S\right|})\right\}
. By
Lemma

62
item 1, if
\left|R\right|\neq\left|S\right|
, then
\braket{\phi_{R}|\phi_{S}}=0
. So we may assume
\left|R\right|=\left|S\right|=t.
Then

\displaystyle\braket{\phi_{R}|\phi_{S}}=\frac{1}{2^{3d(n-1)}(N-t)!}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\sum_{f,\sigma}\sum_{b,b^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{t}}\prod_{i=1}^{t}\overline{\bra{y_{i}}H_{f}\ket{y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}}}\delta_{y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}=\sigma(x_{i})}\bra{y_{i}^{\prime}}H_{f}\ket{\left(y_{i}^{\prime}\right)^{\oplus b_{i}^{\prime}}}\delta_{\left(y_{i}^{\prime}\right)^{\oplus b_{i}^{\prime}}=\sigma(x_{i}^{\prime})}\kern 5.0pt.

There are two cases to consider:

•

R=S
. Then by
Lemma

62
item 2 and the fact that

\left|\bra{x}H_{f}\ket{x}\right|^{2}+\left|\bra{x}H_{f}\ket{\bar{x}}\right|^{2}=1

for all
x\in\{0,1\}^{n}
, it is not hard to check that
\braket{\phi_{R}|\phi_{S}}=1.

•

R\neq S.
Now we consider three sub-cases:

–

\mathrm{Im}\left(R\right)=\mathrm{Im}\left(S\right)
, then
\mathrm{Dom}\left(R\right)\neq\mathrm{Dom}\left(S\right)
. Without loss of generality, we can assume that
y_{i}=y_{i}^{\prime}
for all
i\in[t]
.
Fix
i
such that
x_{i}\neq x_{i}^{\prime}
.
For all
b_{i},b_{i}^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}
, if
b_{i}=b_{i}^{\prime}
, then for all
\sigma
,
\delta_{y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}=\sigma(x_{i})}\delta_{\left(y_{i}^{\prime}\right)^{\oplus b_{i}^{\prime}}=\sigma(x_{i}^{\prime})}=0
; if
b_{i}\neq b_{i}^{\prime}
, then by
Lemma

62
item 2,

\sum_{f}\overline{\bra{y_{i}}H_{f}\ket{y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}}}\bra{y_{i}^{\prime}}H_{f}\ket{\left(y_{i}^{\prime}\right)^{\oplus b_{i}^{\prime}}}=0\kern 5.0pt.

Both cases imply
\braket{\phi_{R}|\phi_{S}}=0
.

–

\mathrm{Im}\left(R\right)\neq\mathrm{Im}\left(S\right)
and
\mathrm{BIm}\left(R\right)=\mathrm{BIm}\left(S\right).
Without loss of generality, we can assume that there exists
i\in[t]
such that
y_{i}^{\prime}=\overline{y_{i}}
. If
b_{i}=b_{i}^{\prime}
, then by
Lemma

62
item 3,

\sum_{f}\overline{\bra{y_{i}}H_{f}\ket{y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}}}\bra{y_{i}^{\prime}}H_{f}\ket{\left(y_{i}^{\prime}\right)^{\oplus b_{i}^{\prime}}}=0\kern 5.0pt;

if
b_{i}\neq b_{i}^{\prime}
, then by
Lemma

62
item 2,

\sum_{f}\overline{\bra{y_{i}}H_{f}\ket{y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}}}\bra{y_{i}^{\prime}}H_{f}\ket{\left(y_{i}^{\prime}\right)^{\oplus b_{i}^{\prime}}}=0\kern 5.0pt.

Both cases imply
\braket{\phi_{R}|\phi_{S}}=0
.

–

\mathrm{BIm}\left(R\right)\neq\mathrm{BIm}\left(S\right).
By
Lemma

62
item 1,
\braket{\phi_{R}|\phi_{S}}=0
. ∎

Appendix 0.B
Right Invariance Property

Lemma
7
.

For an arbitrary
n
-qubit unitary operator
G
, we have that

\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\mathsf{PR}\cdot G}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABXY}}}=\left(G_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{1}}}}\otimes\dots\otimes G_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{t}}}}\right)\cdot\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\mathsf{PR}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABXY}}}\kern 5.0pt.

Proof.

We have trivial identities for registers
\mathsf{A}
and
\mathsf{X}=\mathsf{X_{1}},\dots,\mathsf{X_{t}}
.

\displaystyle\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}^{n}}\ket{z}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Id}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}

(23)

\displaystyle\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}^{n}}\ket{z}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{i}}}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Id}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{i}}}}\,,\kern 5.0pt\forall i\in[t]\kern 5.0pt.

(24)

For an arbitrary
n
-qubit unitary
G
and any
x,z\in\{0,1\}^{n}
, we trivially have the following scalar equality:

\bra{x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}G_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\ket{z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}=\bra{x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{i}}}}G_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{i}}}}\ket{z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{i}}}}\kern 5.0pt.

(25)

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\{0,1\}^{n}}\ket{x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{i}}}}\otimes\bra{x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}G_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\{0,1\}^{n}}\left(\ket{x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{i}}}}\otimes\bra{x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}G_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\left(\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}^{n}}\ket{z}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x,z}\ket{x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{i}}}}\otimes\left(\bra{x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}G_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\ket{z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\right)\bra{z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{x,z}\ket{x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{i}}}}\otimes\left(\bra{x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{i}}}}G_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{i}}}}\ket{z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{i}}}}\right)\bra{z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{x,z}\left(\ket{x}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{i}}}}G_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{i}}}}\ket{z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{i}}}}\right)\otimes\bra{z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{z}G_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{i}}}}\ket{z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{i}}}}\otimes\bra{z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\{0,1\}^{n}}G_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{i}}}}\ket{x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{i}}}}\otimes\bra{x}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\kern 5.0pt,

(26)

where we apply
(
23
)
in the first equality,
(
25
)
in the third equality,
(
24
)
in the fifth equality, and simply relabel the variable in the last equality.
Using this identity on each register
\mathsf{X_{i}}
, we can rewrite the expression of
Fact
4.4
as follows. For simplicity, let
\alpha_{N,t}=\sqrt{\prod_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{(N-2i)}}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\ \ \ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\mathsf{PR}\cdot G}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABXY}}}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{N,t}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\dots,x_{t})\in\left(\{0,1\}^{n}\right)^{t}\\
(y_{1},\dots,y_{t})\in\mathsf{DB}_{t}\end{subarray}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\ket{y_{i}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{x_{i}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\cdot G_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\cdot A_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{AB}}}^{(i)}\right)\ket{0}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{AB}}}\right]\otimes\ket{{\{(x_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{t}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{XY}}}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &=\alpha_{N,t}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\dots,x_{t})\in\left(\{0,1\}^{n}\right)^{t}\\
(y_{1},\dots,y_{t})\in\mathsf{DB}_{t}\end{subarray}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\ket{y_{i}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{x_{i}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\cdot G_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\cdot A_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{AB}}}^{(i)}\right)\ket{0}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{AB}}}\right]\otimes\\
&\kern 5.0pt\frac{1}{\sqrt{t!}}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{t}}\left(S_{\sigma}\ket{x_{1},\dots,x_{t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X}}}\right)\otimes\left(S_{\sigma}\ket{y_{1},\dots,y_{t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{Y}}}\right)\end{aligned}

(27)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &=\alpha_{N,t}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{1},\dots,x_{t})\in\left(\{0,1\}^{n}\right)^{t}\\
(y_{1},\dots,y_{t})\in\mathsf{DB}_{t}\end{subarray}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\ket{y_{i}}\hskip-3.99994pt\bra{x_{i}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{A}}}\cdot A_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{AB}}}^{(i)}\right)\ket{0}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{AB}}}\right]\otimes\\
&\kern 5.0pt\frac{1}{\sqrt{t!}}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{t}}\left(S_{\sigma}\cdot\left(G_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{1}}}}\ket{x_{1}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{1}}}}\otimes\dots\otimes G_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{t}}}}\ket{x_{t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{t}}}}\right)\right)\otimes\left(S_{\sigma}\ket{y_{1},\dots,y_{t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{Y}}}\right)\end{aligned}

(28)

\displaystyle=\left(G_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{1}}}}\otimes\dots\otimes G_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{t}}}}\right)\cdot\ket{\mathcal{A}_{t}^{\mathsf{PR}}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{ABXY}}}\kern 5.0pt,

(29)

where in
(
27
)
, we rewrite the relation state on registers
\mathsf{XY}
, using the fact that
\gamma_{R}
in
(
1
)
simplifies to
t!
when all
y_{i}
in the set are distinct. We then apply
(
26
)
to obtain
(
28
)
, and
(
29
)
follows from the fact that
\left(G_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{1}}}}\otimes\cdots\otimes G_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsf{X_{t}}}}\right)
performs an identical operation on all
t
registers.
∎

Appendix 0.C
Proofs of
Lemma
12
and
Lemma
13

0.C.1
Proof of
Lemma
12

Lemma
12
.

\left\{\ket{\phi_{L,R}}\right\}_{L,R:L\cup R\in\mathfrak{R}^{\mathrm{DB}}}
forms a set of orthonormal vectors.

Proof.

Let
L=\left\{(x_{1},y_{1}),\dots,(x_{l},y_{l})\right\}\in\mathfrak{R}_{l}
,

R=\left\{(x^{\prime}_{1},y^{\prime}_{1}),\dots,(x^{\prime}_{r},y^{\prime}_{r})\right\}\in\mathfrak{R}_{r}
,

S=\left\{(x^{*}_{1},y^{*}_{1}),\dots,(x^{*}_{s},y^{*}_{s})\right\}\in\mathfrak{R}_{l}
, and

T=\left\{(x^{\triangle}_{1},y^{\triangle}_{1}),\dots,(x^{\triangle}_{t},y^{\triangle}_{t})\right\}\in\mathfrak{R}_{t}
such that

L\cup R\in\mathfrak{R}^{\mathrm{DB}}
and
S\cup T\in\mathfrak{R}^{\mathrm{DB}}
.
We need to prove:

•

\langle\phi_{L,R}|\phi_{S,T}\rangle=1
, if
L=S
and
R=T
;

•

\langle\phi_{L,R}|\phi_{S,T}\rangle=0
, otherwise.

Here,

\displaystyle\langle\phi_{L,R}|\phi_{S,T}\rangle=

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{2^{3d(n-1)}(N-l-r)!}\sum_{f,\sigma}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b,b^{*}\in\{0,1\}^{l}\\
b^{\prime},b^{\triangle}\in\{0,1\}^{r}\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{l}\langle y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}\rvert H_{f}^{\dagger}\lvert y_{i}\rangle\delta_{y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}=\sigma(x_{i})}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{r}\langle{y^{\prime}_{i}}\rvert H_{f}\lvert{y_{i}^{\prime}}^{\oplus b^{\prime}_{i}}\rangle\delta_{{y^{\prime}_{i}}^{\oplus b^{\prime}_{i}}=\sigma(x^{\prime}_{i})}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{l}\langle y^{*}_{i}\rvert H_{f}\lvert{y^{*}_{i}}^{\oplus b^{*}_{i}}\rangle\delta_{{y^{*}}_{i}^{\oplus b^{*}_{i}}=\sigma(x^{*}_{i})}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{r}\bra{{y^{\triangle}_{i}}^{\oplus b^{\triangle}_{i}}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{{y^{\triangle}}_{i}}\delta_{{y^{\triangle}_{i}}^{\oplus b^{\triangle}_{i}}=\sigma(x^{\triangle}_{i})}\kern 5.0pt.

(30)

Case 1:
L=S=\left\{(x_{1},y_{1}),\dots,(x_{l},y_{l})\right\}
and
R=T=\left\{(x^{\prime}_{1},y^{\prime}_{1}),\dots,(x^{\prime}_{r},y^{\prime}_{r})\right\}
.

Note that

\displaystyle\langle\phi_{L,R}|\phi_{S,T}\rangle=

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{2^{3d(n-1)}(N-l-r)!}\sum_{f,\sigma}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b,b^{*}\in\{0,1\}^{l}\\
b^{\prime},b^{\triangle}\in\{0,1\}^{r}\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{l}\langle y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}\rvert H_{f}^{\dagger}\lvert y_{i}\rangle\delta_{y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}=\sigma(x_{i})}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{r}\langle{y^{\prime}_{i}}\rvert H_{f}\lvert{y^{\prime}_{i}}^{\oplus b^{\prime}_{i}}\rangle\delta_{{y^{\prime}_{i}}^{\oplus b^{\prime}_{i}}=\sigma(x^{\prime}_{i})}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{l}\langle y_{i}\rvert H_{f}\lvert y_{i}^{\oplus b^{*}_{i}}\rangle\delta_{y_{i}^{\oplus b^{*}_{i}}=\sigma(x_{i})}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{r}\langle{y^{\prime}_{i}}^{\oplus b^{\triangle}_{i}}\rvert H_{f}^{\dagger}\lvert y^{\prime}_{i}\rangle\delta_{{y^{\prime}_{i}}^{\oplus b^{\triangle}_{i}}=\sigma(x^{\prime}_{i})}\kern 5.0pt.

If
b_{i}\neq b^{*}_{i}
, then
\delta_{y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}=\sigma(x_{i})}\cdot\delta_{y_{i}^{\oplus b^{*}_{i}}=\sigma(x_{i})}=0
. Similarly, if
b^{\prime}_{i}\neq b^{\triangle}_{i}
, then
\delta_{y_{i}^{\oplus b^{\prime}_{i}}=\sigma(x_{i})}\cdot\delta_{y_{i}^{\oplus b^{\triangle}_{i}}=\sigma(x_{i})}=0
.
Therefore, the term in the summation is zero whenever
b\neq b^{*}
or
b^{\prime}\neq b^{\triangle}
. Thus,

\displaystyle\langle

\displaystyle\phi_{L,R}|\phi_{S,T}\rangle=~\frac{1}{2^{3d(n-1)}(N-l-r)!}\sum_{f,\sigma}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\{0,1\}^{l}\\
b^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{r}\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{l}\langle y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}\rvert H_{f}^{\dagger}\lvert y_{i}\rangle\delta_{y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}=\sigma(x_{i})}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{r}\langle{y^{\prime}_{i}}\rvert H_{f}\lvert{y^{\prime}_{i}}^{\oplus b^{\prime}_{i}}\rangle\delta_{{y^{\prime}_{i}}^{\oplus b^{\prime}_{i}}=\sigma(x^{\prime}_{i})}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{l}\langle y_{i}\rvert H_{f}\lvert y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}\rangle\delta_{y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}=\sigma(x_{i})}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{r}\langle{y^{\prime}_{i}}^{\oplus b^{\prime}_{i}}\rvert H_{f}^{\dagger}\lvert y^{\prime}_{i}\rangle\delta_{{y^{\prime}_{i}}^{\oplus b^{\prime}_{i}}=\sigma(x^{\prime}_{i})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{2^{3d(n-1)}(N-l-r)!}\sum_{f,\sigma}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\{0,1\}^{l}\\
b^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{r}\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{l}\langle y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}\rvert H_{f}^{\dagger}\lvert y_{i}\rangle\langle y_{i}\rvert H_{f}\lvert y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{r}\langle{y^{\prime}_{i}}\rvert H_{f}\lvert{y^{\prime}_{i}}^{\oplus b^{\prime}_{i}}\rangle\langle{y^{\prime}_{i}}^{\oplus b^{\prime}_{i}}\rvert H_{f}^{\dagger}\lvert y^{\prime}_{i}\rangle\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{l}\delta_{y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}=\sigma(x_{i})}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{r}\delta_{{y^{\prime}_{i}}^{\oplus b^{\prime}_{i}}=\sigma(x^{\prime}_{i})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\E_{f}\biggl[\frac{1}{(N-l-r)!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\{0,1\}^{l}\\
b^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{r}\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{l}\langle y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}\rvert H_{f}^{\dagger}\lvert y_{i}\rangle\langle y_{i}\rvert H_{f}\lvert y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}\rangle

\displaystyle\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{r}\langle{y^{\prime}_{i}}\rvert H_{f}\lvert{y^{\prime}_{i}}^{\oplus b^{\prime}_{i}}\rangle\langle{y^{\prime}_{i}}^{\oplus b^{\prime}_{i}}\rvert H_{f}^{\dagger}\lvert y^{\prime}_{i}\rangle\cdot\sum_{\sigma}\prod_{i=1}^{l}\delta_{y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}=\sigma(x_{i})}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{r}\delta_{{y^{\prime}_{i}}^{\oplus b^{\prime}_{i}}=\sigma(x^{\prime}_{i})}\biggr]\kern 5.0pt.

For fixed
b,b^{\prime}
,
y_{i}^{\oplus b^{\prime}_{i}}
and
{y^{\prime}}_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}
are distinct since
y_{i}
and
y^{\prime}_{i}
are from different blocks. Moreover,

x_{i}
and
x^{\prime}_{i}
are distinct. Thus,

\sum_{\sigma}\prod_{i=1}^{l}\delta_{y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}=\sigma(x_{i})}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{r}\delta_{{y^{\prime}_{i}}^{\oplus b^{\prime}_{i}}=\sigma(x^{\prime}_{i})}=(N-l-r)!\kern 5.0pt.

Then, we have

\displaystyle\langle\phi_{L,R}|\phi_{S,T}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E_{f}\biggl[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in\{0,1\}^{l}\\
b^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{r}\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{l}\bra{y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y_{i}}\bra{y_{i}}H_{f}\ket{y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}}\prod_{i=1}^{r}\bra{{y^{\prime}_{i}}}H_{f}\ket{{y^{\prime}_{i}}^{\oplus b^{\prime}_{i}}}\bra{{y^{\prime}_{i}}^{\oplus b^{\prime}_{i}}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y^{\prime}_{i}}\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{l}\E_{f}\biggl[\sum_{b_{i}\in\left\{0,1\right\}}\bra{y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y_{i}}\bra{y_{i}}H_{f}\ket{y_{i}^{\oplus b_{i}}}\biggr]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{r}\E_{f}\biggl[\sum_{b^{\prime}_{i}\in\left\{0,1\right\}}\bra{{y^{\prime}_{i}}}H_{f}\ket{{y^{\prime}_{i}}^{\oplus b^{\prime}_{i}}}\bra{{y^{\prime}_{i}}^{\oplus b^{\prime}_{i}}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y^{\prime}_{i}}\biggr]

Note that for
y\in\{0,1\}^{n}
,

\displaystyle\E_{f}\biggl[\bra{y}H_{f}\ket{y}\bra{y}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y}+\bra{y}H_{f}\ket{\overline{y}}\bra{\overline{y}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y}\biggr]=1\kern 5.0pt.

Therefore, we have
\langle\phi_{L,R}|\phi_{S,T}\rangle=1
.

Case 2:
L\neq S
or
R\neq T
.

•

Case 2.1:

\mathrm{BIm}\left(L\cup R\right)\neq\mathrm{BIm}\left(S\cup T\right)
. Without loss of generality, we assume that there exists a
y
such that
y\in\left\{y_{1},\dots,y_{l},y^{\prime}_{1},\dots,y^{\prime}_{r}\right\}
and
y\notin\mathrm{BIm}\left(S\cup T\right)
.
It is easy to check

\langle\phi_{L,R}|\phi_{S,T}\rangle=0
, since

\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\end{subarray}}\!\left[\bra{y}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y}\right]=\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\end{subarray}}\!\left[\bra{\overline{y}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y}\right]=\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\end{subarray}}\!\left[\bra{y}H_{f}\ket{y}\right]=\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\end{subarray}}\!\left[\bra{y}H_{f}\ket{\overline{y}}\right]=0\kern 5.0pt.

•

Case 2.2:

\mathrm{BIm}\left(L\cup R\right)=\mathrm{BIm}\left(S\cup T\right)
.

–

Case 2.2.1:

\mathrm{BIm}\left(L\right)\neq\mathrm{BIm}\left(S\right)
.
Without loss of generality,
we assume that
y_{1}
and
\overline{y_{1}}
are not in
S
. This means

y_{1}\in\left\{y^{\triangle}_{1},\dots,y^{\triangle}_{t}\right\}
or

\overline{y_{1}}\in\left\{y^{\triangle}_{1},\dots,y^{\triangle}_{t}\right\}
.

*

Suppose that
y_{1}\in\left\{y^{\triangle}_{1},\dots,y^{\triangle}_{t}\right\}
.
Without loss of generality, we can assume
y_{1}=y^{\triangle}_{1}
.
We have two cases:

·

x_{1}=x^{\triangle}_{1}
. In this case, the term in the summation
in Eq.
(
30
)
is zero when
b_{1}\neq b^{\triangle}_{1}
.
It is not hard to check

\langle\phi_{L,R}|\phi_{S,T}\rangle=0
,
since

\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\end{subarray}}\!\left[\bra{y_{1}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y_{1}}\bra{y^{\triangle}_{1}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y^{\triangle}_{1}}\right]=\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\end{subarray}}\!\left[\bra{\overline{y_{1}}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y_{1}}\bra{\overline{y^{\triangle}_{1}}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y^{\triangle}_{1}}\right]=0\kern 5.0pt.

·

x_{1}\neq x^{\triangle}_{1}
. In this case, the term in the summation
in Eq.
(
30
)
is zero when
b_{1}=b^{\triangle}_{1}
.
It is not hard to check

\langle\phi_{L,R}|\phi_{S,T}\rangle=0
,
since

\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\end{subarray}}\!\left[\bra{y_{1}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y_{1}}\bra{\overline{y^{\triangle}_{1}}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y^{\triangle}_{1}}\right]=\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\end{subarray}}\!\left[\bra{\overline{y_{1}}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y_{1}}\bra{y^{\triangle}_{1}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y^{\triangle}_{1}}\right]=0\kern 5.0pt.

*

Suppose that
\overline{y_{1}}\in\left\{y^{\triangle}_{1},\dots,y^{\triangle}_{t}\right\}
.
Without loss of generality, we can assume
\overline{y_{1}}=y^{\triangle}_{1}
.
We have two cases:

·

x_{1}=x^{\triangle}_{1}
. In this case, the term in the summation in Eq.
(
30
)
is zero when
b_{1}=b^{\triangle}_{1}
. It is not hard to check

\langle\phi_{L,R}|\phi_{S,T}\rangle=0
,
since

\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\end{subarray}}\!\left[\bra{y_{1}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y_{1}}\bra{\overline{y^{\triangle}_{1}}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y^{\triangle}_{1}}\right]=\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\end{subarray}}\!\left[\bra{\overline{y_{1}}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y_{1}}\bra{y^{\triangle}_{1}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y^{\triangle}_{1}}\right]=0\kern 5.0pt.

·

x_{1}\neq x^{\triangle}_{1}
. In this case, the term in the summation in Eq.
(
30
)
is zero when
b_{1}\neq b^{\triangle}_{1}
. It is not hard to check

\langle\phi_{L,R}|\phi_{S,T}\rangle=0
,
since

\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\end{subarray}}\!\left[\bra{y_{1}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y_{1}}\bra{y^{\triangle}_{1}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y^{\triangle}_{1}}+\bra{\overline{y_{1}}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y_{1}}\bra{\overline{y^{\triangle}_{1}}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y^{\triangle}_{1}}\right]=0\kern 5.0pt.

–

Case 2.2.2:

\mathrm{BIm}\left(L\right)=\mathrm{BIm}\left(S\right)
. This means
\mathrm{BIm}\left(R\right)=\mathrm{BIm}\left(T\right)
as well.

*

Case 2.2.2.1:

\left\{y_{1},\dots,y_{l}\right\}\neq\left\{y^{*}_{1},\dots,y^{*}_{s}\right\}
.
Without loss of generality, we can assume
y_{1}=\overline{y^{*}_{1}}
.
We have two cases:

·

x_{1}=x^{*}_{1}
. In this case, the term in the summation in Eq.
(
30
)
is zero when
b_{1}=b^{*}_{1}
. It is not hard to check

\langle\phi_{L,R}|\phi_{S,T}\rangle=0
,
since

\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\end{subarray}}\!\left[\bra{y_{1}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y_{1}}\bra{y^{*}_{1}}H_{f}\ket{\overline{y^{*}_{1}}}\right]=\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\end{subarray}}\!\left[\bra{\overline{y_{1}}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y_{1}}\bra{y^{*}_{1}}H_{f}\ket{y^{*}_{1}}\right]=0\kern 5.0pt.

·

x_{1}\neq x^{*}_{1}
. In this case, the term in the summation in Eq.
(
30
)
is zero when
b_{1}\neq b^{*}_{1}
. It is not hard to check

\langle\phi_{L,R}|\phi_{S,T}\rangle=0
,
since

\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\end{subarray}}\!\left[\bra{y_{1}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y_{1}}\bra{y^{*}_{1}}H_{f}\ket{y^{*}_{1}}\right]=\E_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\end{subarray}}\!\left[\bra{\overline{y_{1}}}H_{f}^{\dagger}\ket{y_{1}}\bra{y^{*}_{1}}H_{f}\ket{\overline{y^{*}_{1}}}\right]=0\kern 5.0pt.

*

Case 2.2.2.2:

\left\{y^{\prime}_{1},\dots,y^{\prime}_{r}\right\}\neq\left\{y^{\triangle}_{1},\dots,y^{\triangle}_{t}\right\}
. Similar to Case 2.2.2.1, we have
\langle\phi_{L,R}|\phi_{S,T}\rangle=0
.

*

Case 2.2.2.3:

\left\{y_{1},\dots,y_{l}\right\}=\left\{y^{*}_{1},\dots,y^{*}_{s}\right\}
and
\left\{y^{\prime}_{1},\dots,y^{\prime}_{r}\right\}=\{y^{\triangle}_{1},

\dots,y^{\triangle}_{t}\}
.

·

Suppose
L\neq S
. Then there exist
(x,y)\in L
and
(x^{*},y)\in S
such that
x\neq x^{*}
.
Without loss of generality, we can assume
y_{1}=y^{*}_{1}
and
x_{1}\neq x^{*}_{1}
. In this case, the term in the summation in Eq.
(
30
)
is zero when
b_{1}=b^{*}_{1}
. It is not hard to check

\langle\phi_{L,R}|\phi_{S,T}\rangle=0
,
since
